| PR-0190 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[56] | New York is much _____ than my hometown. | bigger | more big · more bigger · biggest | 'Much' intensifica un comparativo; el adjetivo sigue tomando '-er': much bigger. | 'Much' intensifies a comparative; the adjective still takes '-er': much bigger. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0191 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[57] | This test was a little _____ than the last one. | easier | more easy · more easier · easiest | 'A little' suaviza un comparativo; los adjetivos de dos sílabas en 'y' usan '-ier': easier. | 'A little' softens a comparative; two-syllable adjectives ending in 'y' use '-ier': easier. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0192 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[58] | She has _____ patience than her sister. | more | much · many · most | Con sustantivos incontables, el comparativo de cantidad es 'more' (more patience). | For uncountable nouns, the comparative of quantity is 'more' (more patience). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0193 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[59] | There were _____ people at the concert than we expected. | fewer | less · least · fewest | Se usa 'fewer' con sustantivos contables (people); 'less' es para incontables. | Use 'fewer' with countable nouns (people); 'less' is for uncountable nouns. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0194 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[60] | I earn _____ money now than at my old job. | less | fewer · least · little | Se usa 'less' con incontables como 'money'; 'fewer' es para contables. | Use 'less' with uncountable nouns like 'money'; 'fewer' is for countables. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0195 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[61] | The weather today is _____ as it was yesterday — exactly the same. | just as nice | as nicer · just nicer · as nice than | 'Just as ... as' enfatiza igualdad exacta: just as nice as. | 'Just as ... as' emphasizes exact equality: just as nice as. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0196 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[62] | That hotel wasn't _____ we thought it would be. | as good as | as well as · so good than · good as | La calidad se compara con el adjetivo 'good' en 'as good as', no con el adverbio 'well'. | Compare quality with the adjective 'good' in 'as good as', not the adverb 'well'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0197 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[63] | This is one of the _____ decisions I've ever had to make. | most difficult | more difficult · difficultest · difficulter | 'One of the + superlativo + sustantivo plural' requiere el superlativo 'most difficult'. | 'One of the + superlative + plural noun' requires the superlative 'most difficult'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0198 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[64] | The report is due tomorrow, so this is the _____ possible moment to start. | latest | most late · later · last | 'Late' es un adjetivo corto: latest (superlativo). 'Latest' también significa 'más reciente'. | 'Late' is a short adjective: latest (superlative). 'Latest' also means 'most recent'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0199 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[65] | The economy is in a _____ state than it was a decade ago. | worse | more bad · worst · badder | El comparativo de 'bad' es el irregular 'worse'; 'worst' es el superlativo. | Comparative of 'bad' is the irregular 'worse'; 'worst' is the superlative. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0200 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[66] | The more complex the system becomes, the _____ it is to maintain. | more expensive | expensiver · most expensive · expensively | En el patrón de comparativo doble, ambas cláusulas usan 'the more/-er'; los adjetivos largos mantienen 'more'. | In the double-comparative pattern, both clauses use 'the more/-er'; long adjectives keep 'more'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0201 | enUsForEs | gram | C2 | gram[67] | Far from improving, the situation grew _____ with each passing hour. | ever more precarious | ever most precarious · even more precariously · more precariouser | 'Ever more + adjetivo' es una forma culta de expresar un grado comparativo que sube sin cesar. | 'Ever more + adjective' is a formal way to express a steadily rising comparative degree. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0202 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[68] | If you heat ice, it _____ into water. | turns | will turn · would turn · turned | Condicional cero para hechos generales: 'if' + presente simple, presente simple. | Zero conditional for general facts: 'if' + present simple, present simple. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0203 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[69] | Water boils _____ you heat it to 100 degrees Celsius. | if | unless · would · did | El condicional cero con 'if' introduce la condición de un hecho científico. | Zero conditional 'if' introduces the condition for a scientific fact. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0204 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[70] | If it rains tomorrow, we _____ the picnic. | will cancel | cancel · would cancel · cancelled | Primer condicional para un futuro probable: 'if' + presente, 'will' + verbo base. | First conditional for a likely future: 'if' + present, 'will' + base verb. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0205 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[71] | If you _____ hard, you will pass the exam. | study | will study · studied · would study | En el primer condicional, la cláusula con 'if' usa presente simple, no 'will'. | In the first conditional, the 'if' clause uses present simple, not 'will'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0206 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[72] | She'll call you _____ she arrives at the airport. | when | if · unless · would | Se usa 'when' para algo seguro; 'if' sugeriría que es incierto. | Use 'when' for something certain to happen; 'if' would suggest it's uncertain. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0207 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[73] | If I _____ rich, I would travel around the world. | were | am · will be · had been | Segundo condicional para presente irreal: 'if' + pasado (were), 'would' + verbo base. | Second conditional for unreal present: 'if' + past (were), 'would' + base verb. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0208 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[74] | If she had more time, she _____ learn another language. | would | will · would have · had | La cláusula principal del segundo condicional lleva 'would' + verbo base para una hipótesis. | Second conditional main clause takes 'would' + base verb for a hypothetical. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0209 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[75] | If I _____ you, I would apologize to her. | were | was · am · would be | En consejos con 'if I ___ you', el inglés americano estándar usa 'were'. | In advice with 'if I ___ you', standard American English uses 'were'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0210 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[76] | If they had left earlier, they _____ the train. | would have caught | would catch · will catch · had caught | Tercer condicional para pasado irreal: 'if' + pasado perfecto, 'would have' + participio. | Third conditional for unreal past: 'if' + past perfect, 'would have' + past participle. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0211 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[77] | If you _____ told me, I could have helped you. | had | have · would have · did | La cláusula 'if' del tercer condicional usa pasado perfecto ('had told'). | Third conditional 'if' clause uses past perfect ('had told'). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0212 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[78] | I wouldn't have missed the meeting if you _____ me. | had reminded | reminded · would remind · have reminded | Tercer condicional: la cláusula 'if' necesita pasado perfecto tras un resultado con 'would have'. | Third conditional: the 'if' clause needs past perfect after a 'would have' result. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0213 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[79] | If I had studied medicine, I _____ a doctor now. | would be | would have been · will be · am | Condicional mixto: condición pasada ('had studied') con resultado presente ('would be'). | Mixed conditional: past condition ('had studied') with a present result ('would be'). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0214 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[80] | If she weren't so shy, she _____ the job yesterday. | would have taken | would take · will take · had taken | Condicional mixto: condición irreal presente con resultado pasado ('would have taken'). | Mixed conditional: present unreal condition with a past result ('would have taken'). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0215 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[81] | You won't pass _____ you study harder. | unless | if · when · until | 'Unless' significa 'if not' — expresa la única condición que evita reprobar. | 'Unless' means 'if not' — it states the only condition that avoids failing. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0216 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[82] | _____ you hurry, you'll miss the bus. | Unless | If · When · While | 'Unless you hurry' = 'if you don't hurry' — la condición negativa. | 'Unless you hurry' = 'if you don't hurry' — the negative condition. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0217 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[83] | Choose the correct sentence about a general truth. | If you drop something, it falls. | If you drop something, it will falls. · If you would drop something, it falls. · If you dropped something, it falls. | El condicional cero usa presente simple en ambas cláusulas para una verdad general. | Zero conditional uses present simple in both clauses for a general truth. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0218 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[84] | If my phone _____, I would have called you back. | hadn't died | didn't die · wouldn't die · doesn't die | El tercer condicional necesita pasado perfecto en la cláusula 'if': 'hadn't died'. | Third conditional needs past perfect in the 'if' clause: 'hadn't died'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0219 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[85] | If we _____ enough money, we'd buy that house. | had | have · would have · will have | Segundo condicional: la condición irreal presente usa pasado simple ('had'). | Second conditional: unreal present condition uses past simple ('had'). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0220 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[86] | Call me _____ you need anything; I'll be home all day. | if | unless · would · were | 'If' introduce una condición posible que puede ocurrir o no. | 'If' introduces a possible condition that may or may not happen. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0221 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[87] | When water reaches 0 degrees, it _____. | freezes | will freeze · would freeze · froze | Condicional cero con 'when' para un hecho inevitable: presente simple. | Zero conditional with 'when' for an inevitable fact: present simple. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0222 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[88] | If I hadn't eaten so much, I _____ sick right now. | wouldn't feel | wouldn't have felt · won't feel · don't feel | Condicional mixto: condición pasada, resultado presente ('wouldn't feel' ahora). | Mixed conditional: past condition, present result ('wouldn't feel' now). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0223 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[89] | If he _____ the truth, he would tell us. | knew | knows · had known · would know | Segundo condicional: el presente irreal usa pasado simple ('knew') en la cláusula 'if'. | Second conditional: unreal present uses past simple ('knew') in the 'if' clause. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0224 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[90] | I'll help you _____ you promise to keep it a secret. | if | unless · would · when not | 'If' establece una condición positiva para el ofrecimiento de ayuda. | 'If' sets a positive condition for the offer to help. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0225 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[91] | She wouldn't be so tired today if she _____ to bed earlier last night. | had gone | went · would go · goes | Condicional mixto: cláusula 'if' pasada (pasado perfecto) con resultado presente. | Mixed conditional: past 'if' clause (past perfect) with a present result. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0226 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[92] | The alarm goes off _____ someone opens the door. | whenever | unless · would · if not | 'Whenever' significa 'cada vez' — un resultado repetido y automático. | 'Whenever' means 'every time' — a repeated, automatic result. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0227 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[93] | If you _____ the button, the machine starts. | press | pressed · would press · will press | Condicional cero para una causa-efecto fiable: presente simple en ambas cláusulas. | Zero conditional for a reliable cause-and-effect: present simple both clauses. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0228 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[94] | We _____ won the game if our best player hadn't been injured. | would have | would · will have · had | Resultado del tercer condicional: 'would have' + participio ('won'). | Third conditional result: 'would have' + past participle ('won'). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0229 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[95] | Don't touch that pan _____ you want to burn yourself. | unless | if not · when · would | 'Unless you want' = 'if you don't want' — la excepción a la advertencia. | 'Unless you want' = 'if you don't want' — the exception to the warning. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0230 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[96] | If I _____ known you were coming, I would have baked a cake. | had | have · would have · was | Cláusula 'if' del tercer condicional: pasado perfecto 'had known'. | Third conditional 'if' clause: past perfect 'had known'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0231 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[97] | If it _____ raining, we can go for a walk. | stops | stopped · would stop · will stop | Primer condicional con 'can': 'if' + presente simple, 'can' en el resultado. | First conditional with 'can': 'if' + present simple, 'can' in the result. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0232 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[98] | If I _____ the lottery, I would quit my job immediately. | won | win · had won · would win | Segundo condicional para una situación imaginada: pasado simple 'won' tras 'if'. | Second conditional for an imagined situation: past simple 'won' after 'if'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0233 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[99] | You can't enter the club _____ you show your ID. | unless | if · when · until not | 'Unless you show' = 'if you don't show' — la única forma de entrar. | 'Unless you show' = 'if you don't show' — the only way in. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0234 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[100] | If humans _____ wings, the world would be very different. | had | have · had had · would have | Segundo condicional para un presente puramente imaginario: pasado simple 'had'. | Second conditional for a purely imaginary present: past simple 'had'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0235 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[101] | I always feel better _____ I go for a run in the morning. | when | unless · would · if not | 'When' encaja con un resultado habitual y regular que el hablante sabe que ocurre. | 'When' fits a regular, habitual result the speaker knows happens. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0236 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[102] | If she had accepted the offer, she _____ living in Paris today. | would be | would have been · will be · had been | Condicional mixto: condición pasada ('had accepted') con resultado presente ('would be'). | Mixed conditional: past condition ('had accepted') with a present result ('would be'). |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0237 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[103] | She is very interested _____ learning new languages. | in | on · at · for | 'Interested' siempre va seguido de 'in'. | 'Interested' is always followed by 'in'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0238 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[104] | He is really good _____ playing the guitar. | at | in · on · with | 'Good at' expresa habilidad o destreza para hacer algo. | 'Good at' expresses skill or ability at doing something. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0239 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[105] | Our plans depend _____ the weather. | on | of · from · in | 'Depend on' es fijo; ojo, el español 'depender de' no lleva 'of'. | 'Depend on' is fixed; note Spanish 'depender de' does not use 'of'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0240 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[106] | Many children are afraid _____ the dark. | of | to · from · with | 'Afraid of' introduce lo que causa el miedo. | 'Afraid of' introduces the thing that causes fear. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0241 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[107] | She has been married _____ him for ten years. | to | with · at · of | 'Married to' nombra al cónyuge; no 'married with'. | 'Married to' names the spouse; not 'married with'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0242 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[108] | I'm not very keen _____ spicy food. | on | in · at · of | 'Keen on' significa aficionado o entusiasmado con algo. | 'Keen on' means fond of or enthusiastic about. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0243 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[109] | This region is famous _____ its beaches. | for | of · by · with | 'Famous for' nombra aquello por lo que algo es conocido. | 'Famous for' names what makes something well known. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0244 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[110] | The result was different _____ what we expected. | from | than · to · of | El inglés americano prefiere 'different from'. | American English prefers 'different from'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0245 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[111] | Wine is made _____ grapes. | from | of · with · by | 'Made from' se usa cuando el material original se transforma. | 'Made from' is used when the original material is transformed. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0246 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[112] | This bracelet is made _____ silver. | of | from · with · by | 'Made of' se usa cuando el material aún se reconoce. | 'Made of' is used when the material is still recognizable. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0247 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[113] | I'm proud _____ what my team achieved this year. | of | for · about · with | 'Proud of' introduce el motivo de orgullo. | 'Proud of' introduces the source of pride. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0249 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[115] | The manager accused him _____ stealing the money. | of | for · with · about | 'Accuse someone of' algo es la forma fija. | 'Accuse someone of' something is fixed. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0250 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[116] | Success often depends on being able to concentrate _____ one task. | on | in · at · with | 'Concentrate on' significa enfocar la atención en algo. | 'Concentrate on' means to focus attention on something. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0251 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[117] | She's responsible _____ hiring new staff. | for | of · about · to | 'Responsible for' nombra la tarea o el deber. | 'Responsible for' names the duty or task. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0252 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[118] | I completely agree _____ you on this point. | with | to · on · of | 'Agree with' una persona; 'agree on/about' un tema; 'agree to' una propuesta. | 'Agree with' a person; 'agree on/about' a topic; 'agree to' a proposal. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0253 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[119] | This song reminds me _____ my childhood. | of | about · to · on | 'Remind of' evoca un recuerdo; 'remind about' es recordar hacer algo. | 'Remind of' triggers a memory; 'remind about' is a reminder to do something. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0254 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[120] | The book consists _____ three separate parts. | of | in · from · on | 'Consist of' introduce las partes de algo; nunca lleva 'in'. | 'Consist of' introduces the parts of something; never used with 'in'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0255 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[121] | Good health depends on a diet rich _____ vitamins. | in | of · with · on | 'Rich in' expresa abundancia de un componente. | 'Rich in' expresses abundance of a component. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0256 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[122] | We congratulated her _____ passing the exam. | on | for · about · of | En inglés americano se felicita 'congratulate someone on' por un logro. | In American English you 'congratulate someone on' an achievement. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0257 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[123] | The company benefits _____ having a diverse workforce. | from | of · with · on | 'Benefit from' significa obtener una ventaja de algo. | 'Benefit from' means to gain an advantage from something. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0258 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[124] | She insisted _____ paying for the meal herself. | on | in · to · for | 'Insist on' va seguido de un sustantivo o una forma en -ing. | 'Insist on' is followed by a noun or an -ing form. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0259 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[125] | You can't rely _____ him to arrive on time. | on | in · of · to | 'Rely on' significa depender de o confiar en alguien. | 'Rely on' means to depend on or trust someone. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0262 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[128] | Please don't shout _____ me; I can hear you. | at | to · on · with | 'Shout at' implica enojo; 'shout to' es solo para hacerse oír a distancia. | 'Shout at' implies anger; 'shout to' is just to be heard at a distance. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0263 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[129] | The success of the trip will result _____ better team spirit. | in | from · of · on | 'Result in' introduce el efecto; 'result from' introduce la causa. | 'Result in' introduces the effect; 'result from' introduces the cause. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0347 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[213] | You _____ drink more water; it's good for your health. | should | must · can · might | 'Should' da un consejo o una recomendación. | 'Should' gives advice or a recommendation. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0387 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[253] | By the time we arrived, the train _____. | had left | left · was leaving · has left | El pasado perfecto ('had left') marca una acción terminada antes de otro evento pasado. | Past perfect ('had left') marks an action completed before another past event. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0388 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[254] | She couldn't get in because she _____ her keys. | had forgotten | forgot · was forgetting · has forgotten | La causa anterior usa pasado perfecto ('had forgotten') antes del resultado pasado. | The earlier cause uses past perfect ('had forgotten') before the past result. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0389 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[255] | After he _____ his work, he went home. | had finished | finished · was finishing · finishes | El pasado perfecto enfatiza que la primera acción se completó antes de la segunda. | Past perfect emphasizes the first action was fully completed before the second. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0420 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[286] | We need to _____ a solution to this problem quickly. | come up with | come down with · come across · come up to | 'Come up with' significa pensar una idea o solución; 'come down with' = enfermarse. | 'Come up with' means to think of an idea or solution; 'come down with' = get sick. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0422 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[288] | The teacher asked us to _____ our homework at the end of class. | hand in | hand out · hand over · hand down | 'Hand in' significa entregar trabajo; 'hand out' = repartir. | 'Hand in' means to submit work; 'hand out' = distribute. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0423 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[289] | The woman _____ lives next door is a doctor. | who | which · whom · whose | 'Who' introduce una cláusula relativa sobre una persona que es el sujeto. | 'Who' introduces a relative clause about a person acting as the subject. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0424 | enUsForEs | gram | A2 | gram[290] | This is the book _____ won the award last year. | that | who · whose · where | 'That' (o 'which') se refiere a cosas; 'who' se refiere a personas. | 'That' (or 'which') refers to things; 'who' refers to people. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0425 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[291] | The car, _____ was bright red, belonged to my uncle. | which | that · who · what | En una cláusula explicativa (con comas), se usa 'which' para cosas, nunca 'that'. | In a non-defining clause (with commas), use 'which' for things, never 'that'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0426 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[292] | That's the student _____ backpack was stolen. | whose | who's · which · that | 'Whose' indica posesión; 'who's' significa 'who is' (quién es). | 'Whose' shows possession; 'who's' means 'who is'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0427 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[293] | The man _____ I spoke to yesterday is my new boss. | whom | which · whose · what | 'Whom' es el objeto del verbo/preposición y se refiere a una persona. | 'Whom' is the object of the verb/preposition, referring to a person. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0428 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[294] | This is the town _____ I grew up. | where | which · that · who | 'Where' introduce una cláusula relativa sobre un lugar. | 'Where' introduces a relative clause about a place. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0429 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[295] | The phone _____ I bought last week already broke. | that | who · whose · where | 'That' puede referirse a una cosa como objeto; aquí también puede omitirse. | 'That' can refer to a thing as the object; it can also be omitted here. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0430 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[296] | Everyone _____ attended the party had a great time. | who | which · whose · where | 'Who' se usa para personas, incluyendo sujetos indefinidos como 'everyone'. | 'Who' is used for people, including indefinite subjects like 'everyone'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0431 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[297] | My sister, _____ lives in Canada, is visiting next month. | who | which · that · whom | La cláusula explicativa sobre una persona usa 'who' entre comas; no se permite 'that'. | Non-defining clause about a person uses 'who', set off by commas; 'that' is not allowed. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0432 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[298] | The bridge, the construction of _____ took ten years, is now open. | which | that · who · whose | Después de una preposición ('of'), se usa 'which' para cosas, no 'that'. | After a preposition ('of'), use 'which' for things, not 'that'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0433 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[299] | The house _____ they lived was very old. | in which | which · that · who | 'In which' es el equivalente formal de 'where'; un 'which' solo dejaría la preposición mal colocada. | 'In which' is the formal equivalent of 'where'; a bare 'which' would leave the preposition stranded incorrectly. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0434 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[300] | The letters _____ every day at 9 a.m. | are delivered | deliver · are delivering · delivers | El pasivo en presente simple es 'are + participio pasado' porque las cartas reciben la acción. | The passive present simple is 'are + past participle' because the letters receive the action. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0435 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[301] | The Mona Lisa _____ by Leonardo da Vinci. | was painted | painted · was painting · is painting | El pasivo en pasado usa 'was/were + participio pasado'; el cuadro recibió la acción. | Past passive uses 'was/were + past participle'; the painting received the action. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0436 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[302] | The new bridge _____ built by next year. | will be | will · is · was | El pasivo en futuro es 'will be + participio pasado'. | Future passive is 'will be + past participle'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0437 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[303] | This report needs to _____ by Friday. | be finished | finish · finished · being finished | Después de 'to', el infinitivo pasivo es 'be + participio pasado'. | After 'to', the passive infinitive is 'be + past participle'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0438 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[304] | The road _____ repaired at the moment, so take another route. | is being | is · was · has | El pasivo en presente continuo es 'is/are being + participio pasado'. | Present continuous passive is 'is/are being + past participle'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0439 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[305] | The package _____ delivered yet; it should arrive tomorrow. | hasn't been | wasn't · isn't · didn't | El pasivo en presente perfecto es 'has/have been + participio pasado'; con 'yet' requiere el perfecto. | Present perfect passive is 'has/have been + past participle'; with 'yet' it needs the perfect. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0440 | enUsForEs | gram | C1 | gram[306] | It is believed that the ruins _____ over 2,000 years ago. | were built | built · have built · were building | El pasivo con una referencia de tiempo pasada usa 'were + participio pasado'. | The passive with a past time reference uses 'were + past participle'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0441 | enUsForEs | gram | C2 | gram[307] | The suspect is thought to _____ the country already. | have left | leave · left · be left | El infinitivo perfecto 'to have left' indica que la acción ocurrió antes; 'be left' sería pasivo, pero aquí 'leave' es activo. | The perfect infinitive 'to have left' shows the action happened before the reporting; 'be left' would be passive, but 'leave' here is active. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0442 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[308] | She said she _____ tired that day. | was | is · has been · will be | En el estilo indirecto, el presente 'am' se convierte en pasado 'was'. | In reported speech, present 'am' shifts back to past 'was'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0443 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[309] | He told me he _____ finished the project. | had | has · have · was | El presente perfecto 'has finished' pasa a pasado perfecto 'had finished' en el estilo indirecto. | Present perfect 'has finished' shifts to past perfect 'had finished' in reported speech. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0444 | enUsForEs | gram | B1 | gram[310] | She asked me where I _____. | lived | do I live · live · am living | Las preguntas indirectas usan orden de afirmación y retroceden el tiempo verbal: 'lived'. | Reported questions use statement word order and shift the tense back: 'lived'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0446 | enUsForEs | gram | B2 | gram[312] | The teacher told the students _____ quiet. | to be | be · being · that be | Las órdenes indirectas usan 'tell someone + to + infinitivo'. | Reported commands use 'tell someone + to + infinitive'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0447 | enUsForEs | gram | A1 | gram[313] | The meeting is _____ 3 o'clock. | at | on · in · by | Se usa 'at' con horas del reloj. | Use 'at' with clock times. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

Someone _____ the window. | has broken | broke · breaks · was breaking | El presente perfecto conecta una acción pasada con un resultado presente visible. | Present perfect connects a past action to a visible present result. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0702 | enUsForEs | trad | A1 | trad[49] | Translate: 'Estoy triste.' | I'm sad. | I'm sorry. · I'm dull. · I'm blue-colored. | El sentimiento simple 'triste' es solo 'sad'; los demas cambian el significado. | The plain feeling 'triste' is simply 'sad'; the others shift the meaning. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0703 | enUsForEs | trad | A1 | trad[50] | Translate: 'Estoy feliz.' | I'm happy. | I'm lucky. · I'm merry. · I'm cheered. | 'Feliz' como estado cotidiano es 'happy'; 'lucky/merry' tienen matices distintos. | 'Feliz' as an everyday state is 'happy'; 'lucky/merry' carry different nuances. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0704 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[51] | Translate: 'Estoy de mal humor.' | I'm in a bad mood. | I'm of bad humor. · I'm on bad mood. · I'm with bad temper. | 'De mal humor' es la frase fija 'in a bad mood', con 'in a', no 'of'. | 'De mal humor' is the fixed phrase 'in a bad mood', using 'in a', not 'of'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0705 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[52] | Translate: 'Es pan comido.' | It's a piece of cake. | It's eaten bread. · It's a slice of bread. · It's a piece of pie. | 'Piece of cake' significa muy fácil; el literal 'eaten bread' está mal. | 'Piece of cake' means very easy; the literal 'eaten bread' is wrong. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0706 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[53] | Translate: '¡Mucha mierda!' (a un actor antes de actuar) | Break a leg! | Break your leg! · A lot of luck! · Much crap! | 'Break a leg' es la forma natural de desear suerte a un artista. | 'Break a leg' is the natural way to wish a performer good luck. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0707 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[54] | Translate: 'Me voy a echar a dormir.' | I'm going to hit the sack. | I'm going to throw myself to sleep. · I'm going to hit the bag. · I'm going to punch the bed. | 'Hit the sack' significa irse a la cama; las versiones literales no tienen sentido. | 'Hit the sack' means to go to bed; the literal versions make no sense. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0708 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[55] | Translate: 'Estoy pachucho / medio malo.' | I'm under the weather. | I'm under the climate. · I'm below the weather. · I'm beneath the storm. | 'Under the weather' significa sentirse un poco enfermo. | 'Under the weather' means feeling slightly ill. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0709 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[56] | Translate: 'La pelota está en tu tejado.' | The ball is in your court. | The ball is on your roof. · The ball is in your field. · The ball is on your side. | 'The ball is in your court' significa que te toca actuar a ti. | 'The ball is in your court' means it's your turn to act. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0710 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[57] | Translate: 'Cuesta un ojo de la cara.' | It costs an arm and a leg. | It costs an eye of the face. · It costs a face eye. · It costs an eye and a leg. | 'An arm and a leg' es el modismo inglés para muy caro. | 'An arm and a leg' is the English idiom for very expensive. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0711 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[58] | Translate: 'De vez en cuando.' | Once in a while. | From time to when. · One time in while. · Of time in when. | 'Once in a while' significa ocasionalmente. | 'Once in a while' means occasionally. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0712 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[59] | Translate: 'Estás tomándome el pelo.' | You're pulling my leg. | You're taking my hair. · You're grabbing my hair. · You're pulling my hair. | 'Pulling my leg' significa tomarle el pelo o bromear con alguien. | 'Pulling my leg' means teasing or joking with someone. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0713 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[60] | Translate: 'Se me fue el santo al cielo. / Lo tengo en la punta de la lengua.' | It's on the tip of my tongue. | It's on the point of my tongue. · The saint went to heaven. · It's on my tongue's edge. | 'On the tip of my tongue' significa que casi recuerdas algo. | 'On the tip of my tongue' means you almost remember something. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0714 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[61] | Translate: 'Cuesta demasiado, no vale la pena.' | It's not worth it. | It's not worth the pain. · It doesn't value the sorrow. · It's not worth the sorrow. | 'It's not worth it' es la frase natural; 'worth the pain' es un falso amigo. | 'It's not worth it' is the natural phrase; 'worth the pain' is a false friend. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0715 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[62] | Translate: 'Cuando las ranas críen pelo.' | When pigs fly. | When frogs grow hair. · When the frogs raise hair. · When cows come home. | 'When pigs fly' es el modismo inglés para algo que nunca pasará. | 'When pigs fly' is the English idiom for something that will never happen. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0716 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[63] | Translate: 'Matar dos pájaros de un tiro.' | To kill two birds with one stone. | To kill two birds with one shot. · To kill two birds of one throw. · To hit two birds with a gun. | En inglés es 'with one stone', no 'one shot'. | In English it's 'with one stone', not 'one shot'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0717 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[64] | Translate: 'Llueve a cántaros.' | It's raining cats and dogs. | It's raining by jugs. · It rains in pitchers. · It's raining dogs and cats hard. | 'Raining cats and dogs' significa que llueve muy fuerte. | 'Raining cats and dogs' means raining very heavily. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0718 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[65] | Translate: 'Estamos en las mismas. / Estamos a mano.' | We're even. | We're in the same. · We are at hand. · We're on the same. | 'We're even' significa que ninguno le debe nada al otro. | 'We're even' means neither owes the other anything. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0719 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[66] | Translate: 'Te salió el tiro por la culata.' | It backfired on you. | The shot came out the back. · It fired back at you. · The bullet went out backwards. | 'It backfired' significa que un plan tuvo el resultado opuesto. | 'It backfired' means a plan produced the opposite result. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0720 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[67] | Translate: 'No hay mal que por bien no venga.' | Every cloud has a silver lining. | There's no bad that doesn't come for good. · No evil comes without good. · Every cloud has a silver line. | 'Every cloud has a silver lining' es el proverbio equivalente natural. | 'Every cloud has a silver lining' is the natural equivalent proverb. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0721 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[68] | Translate: 'Más vale tarde que nunca.' | Better late than never. | It's worth more late than never. · Better tardy than nothing. · More value late than never. | 'Better late than never' es el dicho fijo en inglés. | 'Better late than never' is the fixed English saying. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0722 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[69] | Translate: 'Cuesta arriba / a duras penas lo logré.' | I barely pulled it off. | I pulled it off hardly. · I barely pulled it out. · I hardly took it off. | 'Pull it off' significa lograr algo difícil. | 'Pull it off' means to succeed at something difficult. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0723 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[70] | Translate: 'Andarse por las ramas.' | To beat around the bush. | To walk around the branches. · To go through the branches. · To beat the bushes around. | 'Beat around the bush' significa evitar el punto principal. | 'Beat around the bush' means to avoid the main point. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0724 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[71] | Translate: 'Se me hace agua la boca.' | It makes my mouth water. | My mouth makes water. · It waters my mouth. · It turns my mouth to water. | 'Makes my mouth water' describe el antojo por comida rica. | 'Makes my mouth water' describes craving tasty food. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0725 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[72] | Translate: 'Cuesta creerlo, pero es harina de otro costal.' | That's a whole different ballgame. | That's flour from another sack. · That's a different game ball. · That's another bag's flour. | 'A whole different ballgame' significa un asunto totalmente distinto. | 'A whole different ballgame' means an entirely different matter. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0726 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[73] | Translate: 'A ojo / más o menos.' | Give or take. | By eye. · Give and take. · More or less by eye. | 'Give or take' significa aproximadamente. | 'Give or take' means approximately. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0727 | enUsForEs | trad | A1 | trad[74] | Translate: 'Poco a poco / paso a paso.' | Little by little. | Few by few. · Step to step. · Bit for bit. | 'Little by little' significa gradualmente. | 'Little by little' means gradually. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0728 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[75] | Translate: 'No pega ni con cola. / No tiene ni pies ni cabeza.' | It doesn't make any sense. | It doesn't stick with glue. · It has no feet or head. · It doesn't glue at all. | 'It doesn't make sense' es el significado natural. | 'It doesn't make sense' is the natural meaning. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0729 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[76] | Translate: 'Cuesta un huevo, pero échale ganas.' | It's tough, but give it your all. | It costs an egg, but throw wins. · It's hard, but put desires. · It costs an egg, so try hard. | 'Give it your all' significa dar el máximo esfuerzo. | 'Give it your all' means make your maximum effort. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0730 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[77] | Translate: 'Se le fue la olla / perdió la cabeza.' | He lost it. | The pot left him. · He lost the pot. · His head went away. | 'He lost it' significa que perdió los estribos o el control. | 'He lost it' means he lost his temper or self-control. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0731 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[78] | Translate: 'Por si las moscas.' | Just in case. | For if the flies. · In case of flies. · Just for the flies. | 'Just in case' es el equivalente natural de este modismo. | 'Just in case' is the natural equivalent of this idiom. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0732 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[79] | Translate: 'Borrón y cuenta nueva.' | Let's wipe the slate clean. | Blot and new account. · Erase and count new. · Let's clean the new bill. | 'Wipe the slate clean' significa empezar de cero, olvidando el pasado. | 'Wipe the slate clean' means to start fresh, forgetting the past. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0733 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[80] | Translate: '¿Me puedes dar una cifra aproximada?' | Can you give me a ballpark figure? | Can you give me a baseball park number? · Can you give me an approximate quantity? · Can you give me a near figure? | 'Ballpark figure' es el modismo para una estimación aproximada, no una traducción literal. | 'Ballpark figure' is the idiom for a rough estimate, not a literal 'approximate number'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0734 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[81] | Translate: 'A grandes rasgos, ¿cuánto costaría?' | Roughly, what's the ballpark cost? | In big traces, how much would it cost? · Broadly drawn, what is the cost? · By great strokes, how much? | 'Ballpark' funciona como adjetivo para un costo aproximado; las versiones literales no tienen sentido en inglés. | 'Ballpark' works as an adjective for a rough cost; the literal versions make no sense in English. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0735 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[82] | Translate: 'Solo dame un número aproximado, no hace falta que sea exacto.' | Just give me a ballpark number, it doesn't have to be exact. | Just give me an approximated number, no need to be exact. · Only give me a close number, exactness isn't needed. · Just hand me a rounded figure, exact is not necessary. | 'Ballpark number' es la forma natural y cotidiana para una estimación no exacta. | 'Ballpark number' is the natural everyday phrasing for a non-exact estimate. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0736 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[83] | Translate: 'Necesito un cálculo aproximado del presupuesto.' | I need a rough estimate of the budget. | I need an approximate calculus of the budget. · I need a near count of the budget. · I need a rough calculation from the budget. | 'Rough estimate' es el término estándar; 'calculus' es un falso amigo (cálculo matemático), no una estimación. | 'Rough estimate' is the standard term; 'calculus' is a false friend for math, not everyday estimates. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0737 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[84] | Translate: 'La constructora hizo las cosas a la ligera para ahorrar.' | The builder cut corners to save money. | The builder did things lightly to save. · The builder cut the corners to economize. · The builder made things easy to save. | 'Cut corners' significa hacer algo de forma barata o descuidada para ahorrar tiempo o dinero. | 'Cut corners' means to do something cheaply or poorly to save time or money. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0738 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[85] | Translate: 'No escatimes en la seguridad de los trabajadores.' | Don't cut corners on worker safety. | Don't skimp the corners on worker safety. · Don't save corners in worker safety. · Don't shorten the corners of worker safety. | 'Cut corners on' advierte contra sacrificar la calidad; 'escatimar' corresponde a este modismo. | 'Cut corners on' something warns against sacrificing quality; 'escatimar' maps to this idiom. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0739 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[86] | Translate: 'Recortaron gastos por todos lados y el producto salió defectuoso.' | They cut corners everywhere and the product came out defective. | They cut expenses on all sides and the product left defective. · They chopped corners around and the product was faulty. · They trimmed everywhere and the product went out broken. | 'Cut corners' capta reducir calidad para ahorrar; 'cut expenses' es más un recorte neutral de presupuesto. | 'Cut corners' captures cutting quality to save; 'cut expenses' is more neutral budgeting. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0740 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[87] | Translate: 'La empresa está en números rojos este trimestre.' | The company is in the red this quarter. | The company is in red numbers this quarter. · The company is on the red this quarter. · The company is into the reds this quarter. | 'In the red' significa operar con pérdidas; 'red numbers' literal no es inglés idiomático. | 'In the red' means operating at a loss; the literal 'red numbers' isn't idiomatic English. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0741 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[88] | Translate: 'Por fin salimos de números rojos.' | We're finally out of the red. | We finally left the red numbers. · We're finally off the reds. · We finally exited red figures. | 'Out of the red' significa ya no perder dinero; se mantiene el modismo fijo. | 'Out of the red' means no longer losing money; keep the fixed idiom. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0742 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[89] | Translate: 'Después de meses de pérdidas, la tienda volvió a números negros.' | After months of losses, the store is back in the black. | After months of losses, the store returned to black numbers. · After months of losses, the store went back on the black. · After months of losses, the store came to the blacks. | 'In the black' es lo opuesto a 'in the red' — volver a tener ganancias. | 'In the black' is the opposite of 'in the red' — making a profit again. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0743 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[90] | Translate: 'Mi cuenta bancaria está en rojo otra vez.' | My bank account is in the red again. | My bank account is in red again. · My bank account is red again. · My bank account went to red again. | En cuentas personales, 'in the red' significa sobregirada o con saldo negativo. | For personal accounts, 'in the red' means overdrawn or negative balance. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0744 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[91] | Translate: 'Alguien tiene que ganarse el pan en esta casa.' | Someone has to bring home the bacon in this house. | Someone has to earn the bread in this house. · Someone has to win the bread in this house. · Someone has to gain the bread at this house. | 'Bring home the bacon' significa ganar el sustento del hogar — el modismo para 'ganarse el pan'. | 'Bring home the bacon' means to earn the household income — the idiom for 'ganarse el pan'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0745 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[92] | Translate: 'Ella es la que mantiene a la familia.' | She's the one who brings home the bacon. | She's the one who maintains the family. · She's the one who holds the family. · She's the one who supports the bacon. | 'Brings home the bacon' significa idiomáticamente ser el sostén económico. | 'Brings home the bacon' idiomatically means being the breadwinner. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0746 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[93] | Translate: 'Trabaja duro para llevar el pan a casa.' | He works hard to bring home the bacon. | He works hard to carry the bread home. · He works hard to bring the bread to house. · He works hard to take home the loaf. | Usa el modismo fijo 'bring home the bacon'; traducir 'pan' literalmente pierde la expresión. | Use the fixed 'bring home the bacon'; a literal 'bread' translation loses the idiom. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0747 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[94] | Translate: 'El plan fracasó; hay que volver a la mesa de dibujo.' | The plan failed; it's back to the drawing board. | The plan failed; back to the drawing table. · The plan failed; return to the drawing desk. · The plan failed; go to the sketch board. | 'Back to the drawing board' significa empezar el plan de nuevo; es una frase fija. | 'Back to the drawing board' means starting the plan over; it's a fixed phrase. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0748 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[95] | Translate: 'No funcionó, así que a empezar de cero otra vez.' | It didn't work, so it's back to the drawing board. | It didn't work, so let's start from zero again. · It didn't work, so begin from scratch board. · It didn't work, so back to the paint board. | 'Back to the drawing board' es la forma idiomática de decir 'empezar de nuevo' tras fallar. | 'Back to the drawing board' is the idiomatic way to say 'start over' after failure. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0749 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[96] | Translate: 'Rechazaron la propuesta, de vuelta a la mesa de dibujo.' | They rejected the proposal — back to the drawing board. | They rejected the proposal — back to the design table. · They rejected the proposal — again to the drawing board. · They rejected the proposal — return of the drawing board. | La frase fija es 'back to the drawing board', sin cambiar el artículo. | The set phrase is 'back to the drawing board', with no article change. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0750 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[97] | Translate: 'Trabaja como esclavo por una miseria.' | He works like crazy for peanuts. | He works like a slave for a misery. · He works enslaved for a poverty. · He works hard for peanut. | 'For peanuts' significa por muy poco dinero; 'a misery' no es inglés idiomático. | 'For peanuts' means for very little money; 'a misery' is not idiomatic English. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0751 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[98] | Translate: 'Ese carro cuesta un ojo de la cara.' | That car costs an arm and a leg. | That car costs an eye of the face. · That car costs a face eye. · That car costs an eye and a leg. | 'Cost an arm and a leg' es el modismo inglés para muy caro; la versión del ojo es solo del español. | 'Cost an arm and a leg' is the English idiom for very expensive; the eye version is Spanish-only. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0752 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[99] | Translate: 'La reparación nos costó un dineral.' | The repair cost us a fortune. | The repair costed us a lot of money. · The repair cost us a big money. · The repair charged us a fortune. | 'Cost a fortune' es la frase natural; el pasado es 'cost', no 'costed'. | 'Cost a fortune' is the natural phrase; note the past tense is 'cost', not 'costed'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0753 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[100] | Translate: 'Vamos a tener que apretarnos el cinturón este año.' | We're going to have to tighten our belts this year. | We're going to have to tighten the belt this year. · We're going to squeeze our belts this year. · We're going to press our belts this year. | 'Tighten our belts' significa reducir gastos; el inglés usa el posesivo y plural. | 'Tighten our belts' means to cut spending; English uses the possessive and plural. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0754 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[101] | Translate: 'Vivimos al día, sin ahorros.' | We live paycheck to paycheck, with no savings. | We live to the day, without savings. · We live day by day, with no savings. · We live from the day, without savings. | 'Paycheck to paycheck' es el modismo para no tener colchón financiero; 'day to day' significa otra cosa. | 'Paycheck to paycheck' is the idiom for having no financial cushion; 'day to day' means something else. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0755 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[102] | Translate: 'Ese tipo está forrado.' | That guy is loaded. | That guy is lined. · That guy is covered. · That guy is stuffed. | 'Loaded' es argot para muy rico; 'lined/covered' literales no tienen ese significado. | 'Loaded' is slang for very rich; the literal 'lined/covered' don't carry that meaning. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0756 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[103] | Translate: 'Pusimos toda la carne en el asador para cerrar el trato.' | We went all out to close the deal. | We put all the meat on the grill to close the deal. · We placed all meat on the barbecue to close the deal. · We threw all the beef on the roaster to close the deal. | 'Go all out' significa dar el máximo esfuerzo; la imagen literal del asador es solo del español. | 'Go all out' means to give maximum effort; the literal grill image is Spanish-only. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0757 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[104] | Translate: 'Nos costó sangre, sudor y lágrimas sacar la empresa adelante.' | It took blood, sweat, and tears to get the company going. | It costed us blood, sweat and cries to advance the company. · It took blood, sweat and weeping to push the company. · It cost blood, sweat and teardrops to move the company forward. | 'Blood, sweat, and tears' es el trío fijo en inglés para un gran esfuerzo. | 'Blood, sweat, and tears' is the set English trio for great effort. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0758 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[105] | Translate: 'En los negocios, el tiempo es oro.' | In business, time is money. | In business, time is gold. · In business, the time is gold. · In business, time equals gold. | En inglés se dice 'time is money', no 'time is gold', aunque el español use 'oro'. | English says 'time is money', not 'time is gold', even though Spanish uses 'oro'. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0759 | enUsForEs | trad | C1 | trad[106] | Translate: 'Con esa tienda hizo su agosto.' | He made a killing with that store. | He made his August with that store. · He did his August with that store. · He made a fortune of August with that store. | 'Make a killing' significa ganar mucho rápidamente; 'hacer su agosto' es idiomático, no literal. | 'Make a killing' means to earn a lot quickly; 'hacer su agosto' is idiomatic, not literal. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0761 | enUsForEs | trad | A2 | trad[108] | Translate: '¿Me echas una mano?' | Can you give me a hand? | Can you throw me a hand? · Do you toss me one hand? · Will you hand me a throw? | 'Give me a hand' es el modismo para ayudar; en inglés se usa 'give', no el literal 'throw' de 'echar'. | 'Give me a hand' is the idiom for help; English uses 'give,' not the literal 'throw' of 'echar.' |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |
| PR-0762 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[109] | Translate: 'Tenemos que ponernos al día.' | We need to catch up. | We have to put ourselves to the day. · We must get to the day. · We need to update the day. | 'Catch up' significa reconectar y contarse novedades; 'ponerse al día' no se traduce palabra por palabra. | 'Catch up' means to reconnect and share news; the literal 'ponerse al día' doesn't translate word-for-word. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0765 | enUsForEs | trad | B1 | trad[112] | Translate: 'Rompió el hielo con una broma.' | He broke the ice with a joke. | He broke the frost with a joke. · He cracked the freeze with a joke. · He shattered ice using one joke. | 'Break the ice' es un modismo compartido para aliviar la tensión inicial; se usa 'ice', no 'frost' ni 'freeze'. | 'Break the ice' is a shared idiom for easing initial tension; keep 'ice,' not 'frost' or 'freeze.' |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

| PR-0769 | enUsForEs | trad | B2 | trad[116] | Translate: 'Congeniamos enseguida en la fiesta.' | We really hit it off at the party. | We agreed genially soon in the party. · We congenied at once in the party. · We matched fast on the party. | 'Hit it off' expresa la conexión instantánea; no existe 'congeny' y 'agree' pierde el sentido. | 'Hit it off' captures instant rapport; there's no verb 'congeny,' and 'agree' misses the meaning. |  |  |  |  | data/tracks/enUsForEs.js |

## 5-Decisiones

| ID | Tema | Qué decidimos y por qué | Ejemplo / dónde | DECISIÓN | ALTERNATIVA PROPUESTA | NOTA |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DEC-01 | Qué estamos preguntando aquí | Aquí el español no es lo que se enseña: es la voz que EXPLICA otro idioma. Lo que revisas es si la explicación en español es correcta y natural. | Cursos de inglés para hispanohablantes · glosas del Banco de Palabras de alemán, japonés, coreano, ruso y chino |  |  |  |
| DEC-02 | Necesitamos las dos cosas | Una explicación puede estar en español impecable y aun así ser falsa sobre el inglés o el alemán. Si detectas un error de contenido, dilo: es tan útil como el de idioma. | «'Boring' se traduce como aburrido (que causa aburrimiento)» |  |  |  |
| DEC-03 | Glosas del Banco de Palabras | Son traducciones de una sola palabra. La columna de en medio trae la palabra original y su significado en inglés como referencia; la glosa en español es lo que revisas. | die Zeit (time) → «tiempo» |  |  |  |
| DEC-04 | Registro | «tú» informal, igual que en el resto de la app. | «¿Cómo se dice…?» |  |  |  |
| DEC-05 | Muestra, no revisión exhaustiva | La pestaña de muestra trae unas 40 preguntas por idioma de unas 13.974 en total. Buscamos problemas que se repitan. Si ves un patrón, señálalo UNA vez y lo arreglamos en todo el corpus. | Pestaña 4-Muestra-explicaciones |  |  |  |
| DEC-06 | Idiomas que quizá no domines | No hace falta que sepas japonés o ruso para revisar la glosa en español: la referencia en inglés te dice qué debería significar. Si no te alcanza el contexto, marca DUDA. | Glosas de jaWords, koWords, ruWords, zhWords |  |  |  |
